JULY 1995
14 July 1995
French Republic president urges the inter-
national community to take military 
action to ensure the protection of Bosnian 
enclaves. 
European and US counterparts remain 
sceptical.
14 July 1995
Bosnian Serb forces attack Zepa.
14 July 1995
AFP (France) ‘MSF: Women Refugees in 
Potocari Bear “Visible Signs of Abuse”’.
Libération ‘ “ The World Here Has 
Collapsed”. Médecins Sans Frontières 
Staff Bear Witness from Srebrenica’. 
15 July 1995
MSF Belgium PR
‘Médecins Sans Frontières Conducts 
Relief Operation’.
16 July 1995
Bosnian Serb forces enter Zepa.
16 July 1995
MSF Holland team in Tuzla welcomes 
several thousand people who have fled 
Srebrenica on foot through the woods 
from Tuzla and were reported dead. 
MSF Holland team expresses concern to 
the Dutch minister visiting Tuzla about the 
safety of MSF staff in the Srebrenica 
enclave, particularly the safety of local 
personnel, if the Dutch contingent 
withdraws quickly.
Reporters try to reach the MSF team 
directly on the UNPROFOR battalion’s 
phone line. 
17 July 1995 
Srebrenica: The last patients under MSF 
and the Dutch battalion’s care are finally 
evacuated by the ICRC. 
17 July 1995
MSF Press Releases:
“[…] MSF insists that evacuation must 
include all wounded and local relief staff 
and their Families - MSF personnel still 
present in Potocari likely to accompany 
the wounded. MSF is calling for ICRC 
access to prisoners remaining in Potocari 
and Bratunac to ensure their treatment 
complies with the Geneva Conventions.”

JULY 1995
18 July 1995
MSF Press Conference
Jacques de Milliano, MSF Holland General 
Director in The Hague. 
19 July 1995
MSF teams increase their assistance to 
the thousands of displaced persons from 
Srebrenica who had settled at and around 
Tuzla airport. 
19 July 1995
MSF Belgium/MSF France Press Releases 
‘MSF Asks Pale and Belgrade Authorities 
to Issue the Authorisations for the 
Evacuation of Its Team and 15 Civilians 
from Potocari’.
- ‘A Fourth Plane for Tuzla’. 
NRC Handelsblad ‘Dutch Support Was Too 
Limited,’ ( Jacques de Milliano, MSF 
Holland General Director).
20 July 1995
French intelligence sources tell the press 
that if the NATO strikes on 11 July did not 
achieve their objectives, it is because of 
the absence of guidance on the ground, 
as the British officers responsible for that 
job have left their positions. 
21 July 1995
Ministers of foreign affairs and defence 
and defence chiefs from the 16 countries 
involved in peacekeeping in Bosnia, 
together with Representatives of the UN, 
NATO, and the European Union, issue a 
warning to the Bosnian Serb leaders 
threatening a “substantial and decisive” 
response to any attack on the besieged 
enclave of Gorazde.
21 July 1995
MSF team, composed of two international 
staff  and eight local staff members, their 
families, and two elderly people are 
evacuated from the enclave with the last 
convoy of UN Blue Helmets.
21 July 1995
MSF Belgium/MSF France Press Releases 
‘MSF Team Evacuates Potocari’. 
Trouw ‘De Milliano: “Dutchbat Did Not 
Fulfil Its Promise Sufficiently”’.

MSF Operations
MSF Public Statements 
and Advocacy 
JULY 1995
Dutch Minister of Defence Joris Voorhoeve 
said that he “feared that serious war 
crimes were committed when Srebrenica 
was taken”.
22 July 1995
MSF Srebrenica international team arrives 
in Zagreb with 15 national staff and civi-
lians. 
23 July 1995
Press conference in Zagreb: Colonel 
Karremans, commander of the Dutch 
UNPROFOR contingent in Srebrenica went 
so far as to compliment General Mladic, 
commander of the Bosnian Serb forces. 
Dutch prime minister and the crown 
prince celebrated the efforts of the Dutch 
contingent, congratulating them on “doing 
everything possible to protect the popu-
lation”.
Dutch Minister of Defence Joris Voorhoeve 
reports that the Dutch Blue Helmets saw 
Bosnian Serbs killing a dozen men. 
Some of the UN peacekeepers report 
scenes of extreme violence.
24 July 1995
Tadeusz Mazowiecki, Special Rapporteur 
of the UN Commission on Human Rights 
announces that he lacks information 
regarding 7,000 people, confirmed by the 
Red Cross, and that acts of barbarism 
were committed in Srebrenica.

The Bosnian Serb forces capture Zepa and 
besiege Gorazde.
25 July 1995
MSF brings relief to population fleeing 
Zepa in Kladanj and Zenica.
26 July 1995
MSF Belgium / MSF France Pres s 
Conference, in Brussels, with Amnesty 
International, Causes Communes, and 
Balkanactie, ‘Remember Srebrenica – 
Save Gorazde and Sarajevo – Indignation 
Is No Longer Enough’.

 ‘MSF Treats the First Refugees from Zepa’.

MSF Belgium / MSF France Pres s

‘3,200 People Unaccounted for’, [Zepa].
AUG. 1995
10 August 1995
US presents photographs to the UN 
Security Council proving that Bosnian 
Serb forces executed several hundred 
men near Srebrenica in July. 
UN Security Council calls on the ICTY to 
conduct an inquiry, and also where other 
mass graves are located and calls for a 
report on human rights violations 
committed in Srebrenica and Zepa.

Télérama (France) ‘Srebrenica in Death

Bosnian Serb forces bomb Sarajevo.

Large-scale artillery and airborne offen-
sive from NATO and the RRF.

Message (MSF France in-house newslet-

SEPT. 1995
14 September 1995
De Volksrant: an agreement on the fate of 
Srebrenica’s civilians has been reached 
on 19 July between British General Rupert 
Smith, UNPROFOR’s Deputy commander-
in-chief, and General Mladic, commander 
of Bosnian Serb forces; and the Dutch 
minister of defence, who was aware of the 
agreement, has kept it secret.
Mid-September 1995
Dutch Media requests MSF Srebrenica 
team’s opinion of Dutchbat.
19 September 1995
Ar te ‘ Srebrenica, in Memoriam’, 
Christophe Picard/MSF/EUP documen-
tary.

MSF Belgium/MSF France:
decide that the volunteers can choose 
whether to testify before the international 
courts or not. They will receive all the 
support they feel they need.

MSF France Board decides that MSF 
should carry on questioning the possible

between the Bosnian Serbs and the inter-
national community.
OCT. 1995

MSF statement on the fall of Srebrenica 
criticised the fact that the UN forces in 
the enclave were unable to carry out their 
protection mandate. 
11 October 1995
Christina Schmitz and Daniel O’Brien, MSF 
Srebrenica team respond in writing to 
specific questions asked by the Dutch 
ministry of defence inquiry.

OCT. 1995
17 October 1995
Le Monde (France)  ‘Bosnia’s Column from 
Hell’, Florence Hartmann, quotes from 
‘Srebrenica in Memoriam’, documentary 
produced by MSF France.
21 October 1995
Le Monde (France) reports that during a 
24 May closed-door briefing at the United 
Nations, UNPROFOR commander General 
Janvier recommended abandoning the 
Bosnian enclaves because he considered 
them indefensible.
30 October 1995 
The Independent (UK) reports the same 
information as Le Monde:
USA provides ICTY with new evidence of 
massacres of men in Srebrenica.
UN Security Council calls for an inquiry 
and a report on the Srebrenica events.
31 October 1995
Publication of the report on the investi-
gation commissioned by the Dutch 
ministry of defence: 
 -  clears the UNPROFOR Dutch contingent 
of any responsibility in the fall of 
Srebrenica and the related massacres.
- points to problems in the implementa-
tion of rules governing the intervention 
of UN peacekeeping forces.


MSF and Srebrenica 1993-2003
249
International
The Former Yugoslavia - 
Srebrenica
MSF Operations
MSF Public Statements 
and Advocacy 
NOV. 1995
1 November 1995
Start of talks for the resolution of the 
conflict in former Yugoslavia between the 
Republika Srpska and the Croat Muslim 
Federation, led by the US in Dayton (Ohio).
16 November 1995
On 16 November 1995, the ICTY indicts 
the Bosnian Serb leaders Ratko Mladic 
and Radovan Karadzic for direct, personal 
responsibility for the atrocities perpe-
trated during the fall of Srebrenica.
Richard Goldstone, prosecutor at the ICTY 
is threatening to resign if a deal is struck 
that trades peace for the impunity of the 
Serb leaders.
Mid-November 1995
MSF’s managers decide to give the 
ministry of defence investigators the fax 
exchanges about patient treatment 
between its team and the Dutch contingent 
when the attack on Srebrenica began.
24 November 1995
MSF France Board of Directors is 
concerned about the threat of lifting the 
economic embargo on Serbia as it could 
make it more difficult to deliver criminals 
to the international court. MSF considers 
using the signature of the peace agree-
ments in Paris as an opportunity to air 
the issue in public.
DEC. 1995
1 December 1995
UN secretary general Boutros Boutros-
Ghali presents an initial report on the fall 
of Srebrenica, which sets out “indispu-
table evidence” of a consistent method of 
summary executions in General Mladic’s 
presence at the sites where they took 
place.

MSF Public Statements

DEC. 1995
14 December 1995
Dayton Peace Accords are signed in Paris:
- Lift of economic embargo on Republic 
of Serbia. 
- Creation of a peacekeeping force under 
NATO command (IFOR).
- Creation of state of Bosnia-Herzegovina, 
comprising Muslim-Croat Federation, 
(with Gorazde linked by a corridor) and 
Bosnian Serb Republic.
16 December 1995
Le Monde (France)  ‘Let’s Not Sacrifice the 
Tribunal for the Former Yugoslavia’, by 
Françoise Bouchet-Saulnier, MSF France 
Legal Adviser. 
20 December 1995
N ATO forces (IF OR) replace the 
UNPROFOR.
21 December 1995
UN Security Council demands a more 
detailed investigation of the atrocities 
committed by the Serbs in Srebrenica.
1996
FEB. 1996
February 1996
MSF Belgium/MSF France Repor t 
‘Srebrenica Hospital Personnel and Local 
MSF Staff. Eye-witness Accounts of the 
Evacuation from Srebrenica and the Fate 
of Missing Colleagues’.

International
The Former Yugoslavia -

MSF Operations
MSF Public Statements 
and Advocacy 
MAR. 1996
22 March 1996
MSF team in Zenica asks the organisation 
to consider the possibility of an interven-
tion in Srebrenica, to assist displaced 
Bosnian Serbs from Sarajevo who settled 
there. In the end, the idea is abandoned.
APR. 1996
Early April 1996
The ICTY investigators excavate mass 
graves around Srebrenica.
24 April 1996
Télérama ‘Who Wants Justice?’, by Nicole 
du Roy, quoting Françoise Bouchet-
Saulnier, MSF France Legal Adviser. 
MAY 1996
29 May 1996
British television Channel Four broadcast 
the documentary ‘Srebrenica, a Bosnian 
Betrayal’. It shows that the population of 
Srebrenica was knowingly left by the 
international community to face the 
violence of the Bosnian Serbs.
JULY 1996
4 July 1996
Presentation to ICTY of the results of the 
excavations at Srebrenica, providing it 
with tangible evidence of premeditated 
crimes and direct participation by General 
Mladic.
Former Dutch chief-of-staff accuses the 
French authorities of having forced 
General Janvier to cancel planned air 
strikes before the enclave was captured.
9 July 1996
ICTY issues international arrest warrants 
for Bosnian Serb leaders Radovan 
Karadzic and Ratko Mladic.

11 July 1996
La Libre Belgique ‘Srebrenica One Year On’, 
by Dr Renaud Tockert and Luc Nicolas, 
MSF Belgium.
La Croix ‘Srebrenica One Year On,

French Television channel France 2 broad-

1998

Dutch government commissioned an

UN General Assembly Resolution 53/35 
calls for a ‘detailed report including an

NOV. 1999
19 November 1999
Kofi Annan, the new UN secretary general, 
who was under-secretary general charged 
with the UN’s Peacekeeping Operations 
during the events of July 1995, makes the 
UN’s report on the fall of Srebrenica 
public. It recognises the UN’s ‘errors of 
judgment’.
19 November 1999
MSF France’s Board of Directors decides 
to push for a parliamentary inquiry 
commission on France’s role during 
Srebrenica’s fall.
DEC. 1999
10 December 1999
MSF highlighted the events in Srebrenica 
in its Nobel Peace Prize acceptance 
speech, underlining the need to reform 
UN peacekeeping operations.
21 December 1999
Dutch minister of defence publishes a 
more detailed debriefing report of 
UNPROFOR Dutch Contingent stationed 
in Srebrenica during the events of July 
1995.
Dutch parliament set up a provisional 
commission charged with investigating 
the political responsibilities at play during 
the Dutch peacekeeping operations.
2000
MAR. 2000
20 March 2000
MSF France’s legal adviser presented an 
internal context memo to MSF’s manage-
ment concerning the latter’s call for a 
French parliamentary inquiry commission 
on French responsibilities in the events 
surrounding the fall of Srebrenica.

APR. 2000
12 April 2000
MSF hearing at UN Security Council on 
protection of populations in conflict 
situations. MSF challenges the UN’s 
decision-making processes, which led to 
the abandoning of Srebrenica and other 
places and leaving people without 
protection.
JULY 2000
9 July 2000
The Sunday Times (UK) reports that the UK 
ministry of defence has blocked the publi-
cation of a book by one of the British 
officers tasked with supervising air strikes 
from the ground in Srebrenica.
10 July 2000
UN secretar y general Kofi Annan 
expresses his regret and remorse for the 
attitude of the UN during the Srebrenica 
crisis. 
12 July 2000
A group of Dutch intellectuals publishes 
an open letter calling on their government 
to make a public apology for abandoning 
Srebrenica.
13 July 2000
Le Monde (France): Paul Quilès, chair of 
the French parliament ’s Defence 
Commission, states that, after the 
summer recess, he will be willing to 
examine the possibility of extending the 
type of parliamentary oversight perfor-
med by the commission on France’s 
responsibilities in Rwanda. However, he 
criticised MSF’s appeal as containing accu-
satory biases.
13 July 2000
MSF France Press Release  ‘Médecins Sans 
Frontières Calls for the Creation of a 
Parliamentary Commission of Inquiry into 
France’s Responsibility in the Fall of 
Srebrenica’.
Le Monde (France) ‘Call for a Commission 
of Inquiry on Srebrenica!’, by Jean-Hervé 
Bradol, President of MSF France.

MSF France website on French parliamen-

24 January 2001 
French ministry of defence press release 
justified closed-door hearings for military 
personnel on the grounds that it was 
under the obligation to respect the proce-

During his hearing before the French

mation mission.
24 January 2001
MSF France Press Release ‘Parliamentary

The ICTY’s spokesperson responds that 
its procedures are unrelated to those of 
the French government and that the 
Tribunal has never expressed any reser-
vation regarding the testimony of indivi-
duals it intends to question.
30 January 2001
Jean-David Levitte, former Diplomatic 
adviser to French President Jacques 
Chirac, asserts that no deal was made with

NATO letter to MSF saying that it has not 
received any request to attend a hearing

FEB. 2001
7 February 2001
General Jean Heinrich, France’s head of 
military intelligence from 1992 to 1995, 
then the Implementation Force comman-
der in 1996 (IFOR, NATO-led peace enfor-
cement force for Bosnia-Herzegovina), 
declares to the French parliamentary 
Fact-Finding Commission that: 
- Srebrenica was not defended because 
of a secret agreement between the 
Bosnian Serbs and the Bosniaks. 
- In 1996, IFOR could have arrested Mladic 
and Karadzic on several occasions, but US 
officials had opposed these arrests.

MSF France website on French parliamen-

MSF France website on French parliamen-

JUNE 2001
Early June 2001
MSF France tries unsuccessfully to 
convince the British authorities to allow 
General Rupert Smith, UNPROFOR 
commander in Bosnia-Herzegovina acting 
under General Janvier’s orders during the 
events in Srebrenica, to testify as part of 
the French Fact-Finding Commission.
Early June 2001
MSF France decides to limit its public 
statements on Srebrenica until the 
commission publishes its report, sche-
duled for European autumn 2001.

5 June 2001
MSF France sends documents that the 
programme coordinator referred to in his 
testimony to the chair of the French Fact-
Finding Commission. They did not have 
prior access to them. MSF includes a list 
of other key documents that the organi-
sation has been unable to obtain but 
considers important for facilitation of the 
commission.
9 June 2001
MSF France President of Board of 
Directors’ Annual Report “We are not 
optimistic about the work of the French 
parliament’s Fact-Finding Commission on 
Srebrenica.”
JULY 2001
2 July 2001
After another closed-door session of the 
French parliamentary Fact-Finding 
Commission, General Janvier claims to the 
press: 
- The minutes of the 24 May 1995 meeting, 
revealed by The Independent in October 
1995 and republished on 17 May 2001 by 
MSF are incomplete.
- He recommended keeping observers on 
site and developing strategic air forces.
NOV. 2001
20 November 2001
François Loncle, chair of the French Fact-
Finding Commission on Srebrenica, tells 
Dutch television that General Janvier 
underestimated General Mladic and that 
he should have responded favourably to 
requests for air strikes.

Libération ‘Soldiers – Scapegoats’, by Marc

Le Point ‘Three Questions for Françoise

still far from the truth.”

army chief-of-staff resign following the 
publication of the NIOD report.

19 April 2004
ICTY: 
- Sentences General Radislav Krstic, one

Libération ‘“We Received a Nice Lesson in

Formerly in Charge of Médecins Sans 
Frontières’ Operations in the Former

2007
FEB. 2007
26 February 2007
International Criminal Court (ICC) in The 
Hague rules that the events of July 1995 
in Srebrenica did constitute genocide 
committed by some of the Bosnian Serb 
forces but that Serbia was not responsible 
for this genocide.
2008
JULY 2008
3 July 2008
Naser Oric, Commander of the Bosnian 
Muslim forces in the east of Bosnia-
Herzegovina, and Srebrenica in particular, 
is acquitted on an appeal hearing at the 
ICTY. He had been accused and found 
guilty in a first trial for failing to stop his 
men from committing atrocities against 
the Serbian population and of torturing 
prisoners.
22 July 2008
The Serbian parliament passes a resolu-
tion for a public apology for the massacre 
of 8,000 Bosnian Muslims in Srebrenica 
in 1995.

MAR. 2010
31 March 2010
Ratko Mladic, commander of the Bosnian 
Serb forces is arrested and handed over 
to international justice.
2011
MAY 2011
26 May 2011
Ratko Mladic, Commander of the Bosnian-
Serb forces is arrested and handed over 
to international justice.
2014
JULY 2014
16 July 2014
District court in The Hague finds 
Netherlands liable for the deaths of 300 
Muslims among the 8,000 victims of the 
Srebrenica massacre.
2016 
MAR. 2016
24 March 2016
ICTY finds the Bosnian Serb leader 
Radovan Karadzic guilty of genocide, war 
crimes and crimes against humanity and 
sentences him to 40 years in prison.
2017
NOV. 2017
22 November 2017
ICTY convicts General Ratko Mladic on ten 
counts: one for genocide, five for crimes 
against humanity and four for violations 
of the rules and customs of war. He was 
acquitted of one charge of genocide. As a 
military officer and superior in command, 
he is considered responsible for the siege 
and massacre at Srebrenica. He is 
sentenced to life imprisonment.

MÉDECINS SANS FRONTIÈRES INTERNATIONAL
Route de Ferney 140, Geneva, Switzerland
Tel: +41 (0)22 849 84 84 - Fax: +41 (0)22 849 84 04
www.msf.org
The MSF Speaking Out Case Studies is a series of studies from Médecins Sans 
Frontières (MSF) that openly examine and analyse the organisation’s actions and 
­decision-making process during complex humanitarian interventions focusing 
on dilemmas surrounding speaking out.
The methodology aims at establishing the facts and setting out a chronological 
presentation of the positions adopted at the time. It enables the reconstruction 
of dilemmas, constraints and controversies without pre-judging the quality of 
the decisions made.
All MSF Speaking Out Case Studies are available for download in English and 
French at: www.msf.org/speakingout
© Olivier Jobard/MYOP - Tuzla, Bosnia, July 1995. Refugees from the Srebrenica enclave one day after its fall.
